8	---
9	
10	Decided 2026-06-16: build the Google Workspace Add-on for visitor registration from the Calendar event page **Internal-first** (publish privately to Appointy domain, admin one-click push to all users → no per-user install, no Google verification, no infra). Public Marketplace listing = deferred packaging over the SAME code/engine, earns its verification cost only when a second tenant wants it.
11	
12	Key build constraints to honor:
13	- **Add-on is a dumb surface, not a pipeline.** The engine spine ALREADY exists: `ingestCalendarEvent(NormalizedCalendarEvent)` in calendar-ingest.ts, and `CalendarEventSource` already includes `'marketplace'`. So NO new core fn (the `upsertVisitFromCalendarEvent` name was a placeholder — don't build it). Build only an authenticated producer route `POST /api/visitor/calendar/addon/register`: verify Google ID token → normalize to NormalizedCalendarEvent{source:'marketplace'} → ingestCalendarEvent → enrich passes for write-back. Dedup-for-free on tenantId+iCalUid via calendar_event_links (matches [[project_calendar_visitor_invite]]). Engine spec: office-ops-engine/spec/2026-06-16_calendar_addon_register_endpoint.md.
14	- **Two-way without broad OAuth:** use narrow `currentEventAccess = READ_WRITE` current-event scopes. Attendees arrive in the trigger payload (no Calendar API call); write pass link / "✓ Registered" back to the same event. Broad calendar OAuth stays in the existing ingest pipeline, NOT duplicated in the add-on.
15	- **No CASA:** calendar = sensitive (not restricted). Never add Gmail/Drive scopes — that flips us to restricted and triggers the paid annual audit. Hard line.
16	- **Runtime:** Apps Script (Google-hosted, ₹0) as thin client → HTTPS to office-ops-engine.
17	
18	**Sequencing pushback the user accepted in principle:** research said build the add-on only on evidence hosts forget to add the room resource, and room-resource isn't shipped yet. Plan is designed-and-ready Phase 2 behind a room-resource pilot. Triggers: eventOpenTrigger, eventUpdateTrigger (also Meet-detection for online-meeting guard), action handler.
19	
20	**Home (decided 2026-06-16):** named **"Auxilio Workspace"**, lives in its own subrepo `auxilio-workspace/` at umbrella root with its OWN git repo (sibling to visitor-web/field-app). NOT inside office-ops-engine — runs on Google's servers, deploys via `clasp` to Google Apps Script (not Bifrost/EAS), so the personal-remote/Bifrost rule does not apply. Scaffold (CLAUDE.md, README, .gitignore) created; git init'd; not yet committed.
21	
22	## Vision expansion (user, 2026-06-16) — bigger than a CardService card
23	User wants Auxilio Workspace to be a full Workspace **app** (not just a calendar plugin) taking **per-user calendar OAuth, two-way (read+write)**, surfacing much of the office-ops suite over time. Visitor flow envisioned:
24	- Host creates event → we nudge if it looks like a visitor event.
25	- Fetch all guests → build an editable invite roster the host **fully customizes**: per-guest name, phone, include/exclude (the per-guest opt-out is how the online/offline-mixed-attendees case is solved), pass template, email template, visit location, etc.
26	- Show a **live preview of the invite email** before sending.
27	- Host manages events + invites from the app.
28	- Keep existing behaviors: cancel invites on event delete; cancel-old + send-new on reschedule.
29	
30	Key design decisions reached/implied (confirm before building):
31	- **Card-first (D5, decided 2026-06-16 — REVERSED the earlier web-editor handoff):** the WHOLE host flow lives in the CardService card (roster, per-guest include toggle, name/phone, template + location pickers, email preview, send, manage) via card form inputs + action handlers. **Do NOT bounce the host to a browser tab** — breaks "everything in front of the user," worst on mobile where OpenLink ejects to a browser. The 90% flow fits CardService widgets (text inputs, switches, dropdowns, buttons, images). A full web editor is **deferred/optional (D6)**, built only on evidence the card ceiling is real. Email preview in-card = native summary + send-test-to-self for MVP; pixel-accurate HTML→image preview deferred (infra cost).
32	- **Honest limit:** Workspace add-ons CANNOT auto-pop a nudge (same platform wall as the native tab). Proactive "nudge on create" must come from the **backend calendar-watch** (already have calendar-watch.ts) via Slack/email; in-context card only shows when host opens the panel.
33	- **Visit Draft model (proposed):** a curated roster keyed on iCalUid is the **authoritative source** once a host customizes — passive .ics/OAuth paths then only mutate time (reschedule) or cancel, never regenerate from raw attendees (which would wipe customization). Draft-absent (room-resource only) → auto-create as today.
34	- **Phone not in calendar** → pre-fill from past-visitor history by email, else manual.
35	- **Scopes (D7, rev — protects existing Slack calendar OAuth):** do NOT widen the shared per-user OAuth. It stays `calendar.events.readonly` at CURRENT_SCOPE_VERSION='v2-contacts' — bumping it fires markStaleScopeConnections → reconnect-storms every connected Slack host. Write-back instead uses the add-on's `currentEventAccess = READ_WRITE` (current-event scope, granted at install). Sensitive, no CASA. Echo loop mostly defanged by draft_authoritative gating (re-ingest only retimes/noops).
36	- **Don't duplicate:** reuse visitor-web/ops-dashboard components + renderBrandedEmail (see [[project_email_branding]]) for the editor/preview; add-on deep-links in. Don't build a parallel management UI.
37	- **Forks resolved (2026-06-16):** host editing UI = **the card itself (card-first, D5)** — no web editor in v1. The deferred web editor (D6), if ever built, lives in `auxilio-workspace` (NOT visitor-web/guard PWA, NOT ops-dashboard). **v1 scope = visitor invite creation + management ONLY** — no broader suite, no "today's visitors" homepage panel (North Star, deferred).
38	
39	## NO AUTO-SEND — all calendar paths, effective now (decision 2026-06-17, REVERSES earlier "auto-send stays for room-resource")
40	User chose: nothing auto-sends — neither the OAuth poll NOR the room-resource/.ics path — and turn it off NOW (accepting that calendar→pass is draft-only until the add-on UI+nudge ship). IMPLEMENTED in office-ops-engine: `ingestCalendarEvent` new-event + never-sent-reschedule branches now call `draftFromEvent` (build/refresh Visit Draft, status 'drafted', NO invites) instead of `createInvitesForEvent` (removed). Invites created ONLY via `/addon/send` or the draft-authoritative reschedule (moving already-sent passes). Cancel still cancels sent passes. Removed the redundant flag-gated pre-warm from calendar-sync (ingest drafts for all sources now). OAuth scope untouched (no reconnect storm). Build green; unit tests pass.
41	- **Anti-lag (hard constraint D10):** card-first already removes the worst source (web-app handoff). First card render payload-only ≤300ms (no backend call); **pre-warm the Visit Draft via calendar-watch** so the engine call behind the first interaction hits a warm cache (load-bearing fix — same work as the nudge); each card action = action handler → engine round-trip → re-rendered card, keep ≤~1s; optimistic send (emails/flows async after card returns); debounced preview; OAuth once. Budget: first card render ≤300ms, each action ≤~1s, send feels instant. Test on real mid Android.
42	
43	## Phase 0 engine — IMPLEMENTED (2026-06-17, uncommitted)
44	Landed in office-ops-engine: `addon-auth.ts` (verifyAddonIdToken + isInternalHostEmail, env AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID); `visit-drafts.ts` (Visit Draft store + materializeDraft, phone-from-history pre-fill); `visit_drafts` table + `draft_authoritative` col on calendar_event_links (postgres-schema.ts); five `/api/visitor/calendar/addon/*` routes (draft GET/PATCH, preview, send, cancel-guest) in routes.ts, ID-token authed, reusing createVisitorInvite/renderPassEmail; gated draft-authoritative branch + recordDraftMaterialization/getLinkedInvitationIds in calendar-ingest.ts. `npm run build` green; tsc clean for new/changed files (20 pre-existing errors elsewhere). Did NOT touch CURRENT_SCOPE_VERSION/shared OAuth scope. **Deferred:** pre-warm hook in calendar-watch.ts (lazy getOrCreateDraft covers correctness); regression+convergence tests (NO test harness exists in repo — no test script / no *.test.ts; needs runner decision).
45	
46	## Phase 1 card MVP built (2026-06-17, untested in Google)
47	auxilio-workspace now has the Apps Script add-on: `appsscript.json` (Calendar add-on, eventOpen/eventUpdate triggers, currentEventAccess READ_WRITE, calendar.events.readonly + script.external_request + userinfo.email/openid scopes, urlFetchWhitelist=ngrok domain) + `Code.js` (card-first CardService: roster with include switches + phone inputs, Preview, Save&send; auth via ScriptApp.getIdentityToken(); fetches iCalUID via Advanced Calendar Service so engine dedups). Setup chosen: **ngrok tunnel → local engine** (backendtunnel fixed domain) + **real OAuth token**. Full runbook in auxilio-workspace/SETUP.md (GCP project + Internal consent screen + set engine AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID to the token's aud; clasp create/push; Deploy→Test deployments→Install). Code.js is a first draft written outside Google — refine in the Apps Script editor. Use a DEV/throwaway DATABASE_URL (no-auto-send is live in code).
48	
49	## Lifecycle + manage-screen (implemented 2026-06-17) — plan: Planning-docs/2026-06-17_addon_lifecycle_and_manage_screen_plan.md
50	Better-flow chosen: ONE toggle = "should have a pass" + ONE reconcile (`applyDraft`). Decisions locked: (A) Manage cancel = toggle + "Update passes" button (batched, not immediate); (B) removal auto-cancel on card-open + event-move ONLY (poll never cancels — partial-list safety). Implemented: `applyDraft` in visit-drafts (create newly-included, cancel newly-excluded, keep rest; persists status/invitationId; ledger via recordDraftMaterialization). `/addon/send` + `/addon/apply` both call applyDraft. getOrCreateDraft gained `{reconcileRemovals}` opt — card path (true) drops+cancels removed-sent guests; poll path (false, default) keeps all. Host-name fix: `resolveHostDisplayName(email, fallback)` from staff registry (token has no name claim → was showing email); used in applyDraft/materializeDraft/preview. Card: Manage mode when draft.materialized (toggle on=has pass, status labels, "Update passes"); Setup mode otherwise; onSaveAndSend re-renders via updateCard to switch to Manage after apply. Event move (passive draft_authoritative reschedule) reissues included only; delete cancels all (existing CANCEL path). Build green, 10 unit pass.
51	**STILL OPEN: Calendar.Events.get is unreliable** — it often returns attendees=[] even when guests exist (and sometimes 404s). So the CARD trusts `e.calendar.attendees` (payload) as the source of truth for attendees; Events.get is best-effort ONLY for iCalUID/title/times. Empty-incoming-attendees guard in getOrCreateDraft prevents mass-cancel from a transient empty list.
52	
53	## Refinements (2026-06-17 batch)
54	- **Cancellation emails:** extracted shared `notifyInviteCancelled(invitationId,{cancelledBy,reason})` in invite-manager.ts; called from applyDraft (toggle-off), getOrCreateDraft (guest removed), and calendar-ingest CANCEL (event deleted, refactored to use it). cancelledBy must != 'visitor' for the seed flow email node to fire.
55	- **Empty events instant:** card short-circuits (renders "add a guest" with NO Calendar/engine call) when payload has 0 external attendees — fixes long loader.
56	- **Email template:** add-on uses `visitor.email.pass` key (policies.ts getVisitorUiConfig → getTemplate(key||'visitor.email.pass')) = the admin-editable default. If admin's customization doesn't show, it's because the engine points at a DIFFERENT db than the dashboard. No separate "default selection" feature exists.
57	- **CardService limitation:** can't grey/disable a button on live form change (no client reactivity without per-toggle round-trip). "Update passes" stays enabled with a "No changes" guard.
58	- **Name resolution:** added contacts.readonly + contacts.other.readonly scopes + people.googleapis.com to urlFetchWhitelist. > AGENT

Both fixes are done and verified. Here's what I changed and why.

## 1. Bug: false "Pass sent" + toggle-on for an internal guest

**Root cause** (`office-ops-engine/.../visit-drafts.ts` → `adoptExistingPasses`): when building a draft, the engine tries to "adopt" a pass that already exists for a guest so it doesn't offer a duplicate. It had two matchers — an exact UID match (strong) and a **loose identity match** (`visitor_email` + event date). That loose match ran for *every* guest. Your old event's workspace guest happened to have some active invitation on that date (from an unrelated visit/test), so it got adopted onto this event → `include=true`, `status='sent'`, `materialized=true`.

**Fix:** the loose identity match now fires **only for guests who are visitors by default** (external or different-office — `guest.include === true`). A default-OFF internal colleague is no longer auto-adopted; the exact-UID match still works for everyone.

Trade-off I accepted: in the rare case where a host already made a dashboard pass for an internal colleague *and* toggles them on *and* sends, they could get a duplicate (harmless, not destructive) — far better than the common false "Pass sent" on every normal internal event.

## 2. Show that internal people are internal

Added an `internal` flag per guest (`= !includeDecider`, i.e. exactly the default-off same-workspace colleagues), set in `buildRoster` and re-derived on each open so older drafts get it too. The route already serializes the roster, so no route change.

In the add-on card (`auxilio-workspace/Code.js`) an internal guest now shows:
- a **circular building icon** (Lucide `building` on a muted grey circle, embedded as a data-URI — stays within the Lucide-only / no-emoji house rule) in place of the photo avatar;
- a **"Internal · Workspace"** top-label badge;
- status reads **"Internal — no pass needed"** (grey) instead of a bare "No pass" that looks like something's missing.

The toggle still starts off and the host can still flip it on.

## Verification
- `bun test visit-drafts.test.ts` → 6 pass (added `internal`-flag assertions).
- Added an opt-in integration regression test: an internal colleague with an unrelated same-day pass is **not** adopted (`status='pending'`, `materialized=false`, `internal=true`).
- `tsc --noEmit` clean for the touched files; `node --check Code.js` passes.

Note: `Code.js` changes ship via `clasp push` to Apps Script (not committed/deployed here per repo rules) — let me know when you want to push.

One thing worth a real-device check: the circular building data-URI icon renders fine on the mobile Calendar app (CardService icon images occasionally behave differently there). If it doesn't show, the topLabel + coloured status still carry the "internal" signal.

> AGENT

The new clue — **it still shows "Pass sent" every time you reopen** — points at a second, separate bug from the one I just fixed. Let me explain precisely, then fix it.

## Why it shows "Pass sent" and why it sticks

The card doesn't compute "Pass sent" live — it reads the **stored draft row** (`visit_drafts.roster`) the engine returns. Two things matter:

1. **The status is persisted.** Every code path that sets a guest to `status:'sent'` also writes it into the `visit_drafts` row. So once a guest is written as "sent", it's baked into the database for that event.

2. **The load path blindly trusts it.** When you reopen the event, `getOrCreateDraft` finds the existing row and merges with `{...prev}` — it **never re-checks whether that invitation actually exists, is active, or even belongs to this event** (`visit-drafts.ts:420-425`). So a draft that was written "sent" once shows "Pass sent" forever, even if no real pass is behind it anymore.

So your event got a `status:'sent'` written into its draft row at some earlier point (the most likely writer is the adoption logic I fixed — it grabbed an unrelated active invitation for that workspace person on that date — or a manual toggle-on+send during earlier testing). **My first fix only stops new internal events from being wrongly adopted; it does nothing for a draft that's already written.** That's why yours still shows it.

This is a real robustness gap regardless of the original cause: the draft should re-validate its "sent" guests on load. Let me add that self-heal.

Drop the claim when:\n  //   - the invitation is gone/inactive (any guest), OR\n  //   - the guest is an internal colleague (default-off) whose pass is NOT linked to\n  //     THIS event — the only legit way an internal colleague is 'sent' is the host\n  //     toggling them on + send, which always links the pass to this iCalUid; a 'sent'\n  //     internal guest with no such link is a phantom adoption of an unrelated pass.\n  // Reset to the fresh classifier default so the card reflects reality.\n  const builtByEmail: Record<string, DraftGuest> = {};\n  for (const g of built.roster) builtByEmail[g.email.toLowerCase()] = g;\n  const hasSent = roster.some((g) => g.status === 'sent' && g.invitationId);\n  const linkedHere = hasSent\n    ? new Set((await getLinkedInvitationIds(existing.iCalUid).catch(() => [])).map((id) => id))\n    : new Set<string>();\n  for (const g of roster) {\n    if (g.status !== 'sent' || !g.invitationId) continue;\n    const active = await getActiveInvitationById(g.invitationId).catch(() => null);\n    const phantom = !active || (g.internal === true && !linkedHere.has(g.invitationId));\n    if (!phantom) continue;\n    console.log(`[VisitDraft] guest ${g.email} stored 'sent' but no real pass backs it (active=${!!active}, internal=${!!g.internal}) → resetting to pending`);\n    g.status = 'pending';\n    g.invitationId = undefined;\n    g.include = builtByEmail[g.email.toLowerCase()]?.include ?? g.include;\n  }"
}

> AGENT

## Answer to your "why"

You were right to push — "**it still shows on reopen**" was the tell. There are actually two distinct bugs:

**Why it showed "Pass sent" at all:** the card doesn't compute pass state live; it reads the stored `visit_drafts` row. Your event's row got a guest written with `status:'sent'` at some earlier point. The most likely writer is the adoption logic — it found an *unrelated* active invitation for that workspace person on that date (e.g. from another visit/test) and grabbed it onto this event. The event itself was never used to create a pass — it just inherited someone else's.

**Why it stays "sent" on every reopen (the real new bug):** once `status:'sent'` is in the draft row, the load path (`getOrCreateDraft`) merged it back with `{...prev}` and **never re-checked whether a real pass actually backs it**. So it's stuck forever. My first fix only stopped *new* internal events from being mis-adopted — it did nothing for a row already written, which is exactly what you saw.

## What I changed (this round)

A **self-heal on draft load**: a stored "sent" guest is reset to pending (and back to its correct default toggle) when either —
- the invitation no longer exists / isn't active, **or**
- the guest is an internal colleague whose pass isn't actually linked to *this* event (the only legit way an internal colleague is "sent" is you toggling them on + sending, which always links the pass to the event — so an unlinked internal "sent" is a phantom).

This catches your case **even if that unrelated invitation is still active elsewhere**. I also made `materialized` derive from the healed roster, so the event drops back to "Send passes" mode instead of being stuck in "Manage".

**Net effect:** the next time you open that event (after this is deployed), it self-heals — the guest flips to toggle-off, "Internal — no pass needed", building icon, and the event no longer claims a pass. No manual DB cleanup needed.

## Verified
- Pure tests pass (6/6); touched files typecheck clean.
- Added an opt-in integration test asserting a phantom "sent" guest self-heals to pending + Setup mode on reopen.

Two notes:
- These are engine changes (`visit-drafts.ts`) — they take effect once the engine is redeployed; the `Code.js` badge changes need a `clasp push`. Say the word and I'll walk through deploying.
- If you want a *definitive* post-mortem on exactly which invitation got adopted onto that event, I can run a read-only query against the dev DB to show you the draft row + the invitation it pointed at — but I'll need your OK first (per our no-ad-hoc-queries-on-the-live-DB rule).

> AGENT

Confirmed the real gap. Here's what actually happened, and why my last fix still missed:

On the **first** open (before the adoption gate), the workspace guest got an unrelated active invitation adopted onto the event. Then the draft route, seeing you're the organizer with a "sent" guest, **wrote a `calendar_event_links` row linking that borrowed pass to this event** (routes.ts:656). So my self-heal's "is it linked to this event?" check is defeated — the bad pass *is* linked, and it's still active. That's why it persists.

The robust signal: an internal colleague is only legitimately "sent" if the backing pass was actually created **via the calendar** (`invited_via='calendar'`). A phantom-adopted pass came from another channel. Let me rewrite the self-heal to use that.

196	
197	/**
198	 * Remove a (now-cancelled) invitation id from every calendar_event_links row that
199	 * references it, so a later reschedule can't resurrect it. Belt-and-suspenders for
200	 * the reschedule active-filter (F1). Called when a pass is cancelled outside the
201	 * add-on (e.g. the dashboard cancel button).
202	 */
203	export async function unlinkInvitationFromCalendar(invitationId: string): Promise<void> {
204	  if (!invitationId) return;
205	  const db = await getDb();
206	  const result = await db.execute({
207	    sql: `SELECT ical_uid, invitation_ids FROM calendar_event_links WHERE invitation_ids LIKE ?`,
208	    args: [`%"${invitationId}"%`],
209	  });
210	  for (const row of result.rows as unknown as { ical_uid: string; invitation_ids: string | null }[]) {
211	    const remaining = parseInvitationIds(row.invitation_ids).filter((id) => id !== invitationId);
212	    await db.execute({
213	      sql: `UPDATE calendar_event_links SET invitation_ids = ?, updated_at = now()::text WHERE ical_uid = ?`,
214	      args: [JSON.stringify(remaining), row.ical_uid],
215	    });
216	  }
217	}

> AGENT

Don't wipe the DB — that won't fix the root cause (it'd come back, and it nukes everything else). Here's what's actually going on.

## The real root cause

Your env is fine (`GOOGLE_CALENDAR_DOMAIN=appointy.com`), so the guest *is* recognized as internal. The "Pass sent" is **stale data baked into that event's draft row**, created by a chain I'd missed:

1. An early open (before the adoption gate) grabbed an **unrelated, still-active** pass that workspace person had from some other visit that day, and wrote it onto this event's draft as `status:'sent'`.
2. Because you're the event organizer, the draft route then **wrote a `calendar_event_links` row linking that borrowed pass to this event** — making it *look* legitimately owned.
3. So my previous self-heal (which checked "is the pass active and linked here?") saw ✅ active + ✅ linked and left it alone. That's why it still showed.

The only reliable signal: an internal colleague is only *genuinely* "sent" when **you** toggle them on and send, which creates an `invited_via='calendar'` pass. A borrowed pass came from another channel (`web`/`slack`/etc.). I rewrote the self-heal to key on that, and to also unlink the stale event→pass link.

## What you need to do

**Restart the engine** that your ngrok tunnel points at (the local one — `npm run start:backend`, or `npm run bot:dev` which `--watch`es and reloads on save). My fixes are engine-side; if the running process is the old code, nothing changed — which is the most likely reason it "still shows."

Then **reopen the event once.** On that open the draft self-heals: the guest flips to toggle-off, the building/"Internal" badge appears, "Pass sent" is gone, and the bad link is removed. No DB surgery needed.

If after a confirmed restart + reopen it *still* shows "Pass sent", then my theory is wrong and we should look at the actual row — at that point I'd want your OK to run one read-only query against the dev DB to see that guest's stored `status`/`invitationId` and the invitation's `invited_via`. But try the restart first; I'm fairly confident that's it.

Verified on my end: pure tests pass, types clean, and the opt-in integration test now reproduces your exact case (active + linked + `web` pass) and asserts it heals.

Confirmed the bug. `onSaveAndSend` re-runs `patchFromForm_(iCalUid, e)`, but `e` is the **confirmation card's** event — it has no toggle/name/phone widgets. So `patchFromForm_` reads empty inputs and PATCHes `include=false`, `name=''`, `phone=''` onto every guest, wiping the roster right before send. Result: nothing to send → "No changes", and the draft is left all-excluded → toggle off / "No pass".

> AGENT

Fixed. This one is purely a card (Apps Script) bug — no engine involvement.

## What was happening

The send flow has two cards: the **roster card** (with the per-guest toggle/name/phone widgets) and the **confirmation card** (just the recipient list + an optional template picker).

- On the roster card, `onReviewSend` correctly reads the form and saves your toggles → the confirm card shows "1 pass will be sent." ✓
- But `onSaveAndSend` (the "Send passes" button on the **confirm** card) re-ran `patchFromForm_(iCalUid, e)` — and that function maps over every guest reading the `inc_`/`name_`/`phone_` widgets. **Those widgets don't exist on the confirm card.** A switch that's *off* and a switch that's *not on this card* both arrive identically (absent), so it can't tell them apart and wrote `include=false`, `name=''`, `phone=''` for everyone — wiping the roster the instant before sending.

Result: the send found nothing included → "No changes", and the draft was left all-excluded → toggle off / "No pass". `onPreview` on that same card had the twin bug (it also couldn't find a guest to preview).

## The fix

The roster card already persists everything before navigating, so the confirm card must **not** re-derive include/name/phone. I added `patchTemplateFromForm_` that persists *only* the email-template choice (the one widget that actually lives on the confirm card), and pointed both `onSaveAndSend` and `onPreview` at it. `onPreview` now gets its target guest from a `previewEmail` param threaded in when the confirm card is built, instead of reading switches that aren't there.

Verified: `node --check` passes; call sites confirmed (`onReviewSend` keeps the real `patchFromForm_`; the two confirm-card actions use the template-only patch).

This is an Apps Script change, so it takes effect on the next `clasp push` — not a server restart. Want me to walk through pushing it (and the internal-badge changes from earlier are in the same file, so they'll go up together)?

> AGENT

Confirmed the root cause. Attendees come from the payload (so the card renders fine and shows "will get a pass"), but **event times only come from `Calendar.Events.get`** — the known-flaky call. When it fails (very common for a freshly-created event due to eventual consistency), the draft is stored with `start=null/end=null`. The card still looks healthy, but send fails with EVENT_TIME_MISSING. And once stored without times, the send path never refetches them — so "reopen and try again" doesn't reliably help.

Two fixes: harden the time fetch, and re-resolve + pass times at send so the draft self-corrects.

Returns the event resource or null.\nfunction fetchEventWithRetry_(calendarId, eventId) {\n  var targets = [];\n  if (calendarId) targets.push(calendarId);\n  if (calendarId !== 'primary') targets.push('primary');\n  for (var attempt = 0; attempt < 2; attempt++) {\n    for (var t = 0; t < targets.length; t++) {\n      try {\n        var ev = Calendar.Events.get(targets[t], eventId);\n        if (ev) return ev;\n      } catch (err) {\n        // 404 / transient → fall through to the next target / retry.\n      }\n    }\n    if (attempt === 0) Utilities.sleep(500);\n  }\n  return null;",
  "new_string": "// Resolve the event from the Calendar API, tolerating transient 404s. A freshly\n// created event is eventually-consistent — Events.get can 404 for a second or two\n// after the user adds it — and this is our ONLY source of event start/end (the add-on\n// payload carries attendees but no times). So a miss here means a draft saved without\n// times and a send that fails with EVENT_TIME_MISSING. Retry with backoff (~2.7s\n// total) across the event's calendar and the primary calendar. Returns the event or null.\nfunction fetchEventWithRetry_(calendarId, eventId) {\n  var targets = [];\n  if (calendarId) targets.push(calendarId);\n  if (calendarId !== 'primary') targets.push('primary');\n  var backoffs = [300, 700, 1500]; // waits AFTER attempts 0,1,2 (4 attempts total)\n  for (var attempt = 0; attempt < 4; attempt++) {\n    for (var t = 0; t < targets.length; t++) {\n      try {\n        var ev = Calendar.Events.get(targets[t], eventId);\n        if (ev) return ev;\n      } catch (err) {\n        // 404 / transient → fall through to the next target / retry.\n      }\n    }\n    if (attempt < backoffs.length) Utilities.sleep(backoffs[attempt]);\n  }\n  return null;"
}

> AGENT

Fixed. The "event open, add-on open, still fails" is the giveaway here.

## Why it failed

The add-on payload (`e.calendar`) gives you the **attendees** but **not the event's start/end times**. Times come *only* from `Calendar.Events.get` — the known-flaky call. For a **just-created event** it's eventually-consistent and often 404s for a second or two. So:

- Attendees arrive from the payload → the card renders fine and shows "will get a pass."
- But `Events.get` missed → the draft was saved with `start=null/end=null`.
- On send, `applyDraft` refuses to issue a pass with an unknown duration → "Could not read the event time."

And the send path never re-fetched the times, so the "reopen and try again" advice didn't reliably work — once the draft was stored time-less, it stayed that way.

## The fix (three parts)

1. **Hardened the time fetch** (`fetchEventWithRetry_`): now 4 attempts with backoff (~2.7s total) across both the event's calendar and the primary calendar — enough to ride out a new event's consistency lag.
2. **`onSaveAndSend` re-resolves the times at send** and passes `start`/`end` to the engine, so even if the draft-open missed them, the send carries fresh ones.
3. **The send route applies those times to the draft** before reconciling (and `applyDraft` persists it) — so the draft self-corrects instead of needing a manual reopen.

Verified: engine typecheck clean at the edit (remaining errors are the pre-existing unrelated ones); `Code.js` syntax OK.

## To make these live
- **Engine** changes (send route): restart the local engine your ngrok points at.
- **Card** changes (retry + send-time): `clasp push`.

These pair with the confirm-card fix from the last turn (same file). Once both are deployed, the full flow — create event → add guest → review → send — should issue the pass cleanly. Want me to run the `clasp push` for the add-on, or do you prefer to do it from the Apps Script editor?

Everything checks out locally (syntax OK, clasp 3.3.0 configured). Now the most important point:

## These fixes aren't running yet unless you've deployed them

I can only edit the files — they don't take effect until:
- **Card** (`Code.js`): `clasp push` → then reload the add-on in Calendar.
- **Engine** (`routes.ts` send route): restart the local engine your ngrok tunnel points at.

If you've just been clicking "Send" again in the same tab without pushing/restarting, you're still running the old code — which would fail identically. That's the single most likely reason it's unchanged.

## The error wording will now tell us what's deployed

I made the card produce a *different* message than the engine, so the text itself is a tell:

- **"Could not read the event time. Diagnostic: got=… key=… start=… cal=…"** → this is the **new card**. `got=false` = `Calendar.Events.get` itself failed (a real fetch/permission problem, not a timing blip); `got=true start=null` = the event truly has no start.
- **"Could not read the event time — reopen the event and try again."** (exactly this) → that string comes from the **engine**. Seeing it after deploying means the card resolved times and passed them, but the engine didn't apply them → **engine wasn't restarted**.

So: deploy both, try once more, and tell me the **exact** message. That pins it down with no more guessing.

## Want me to push the card now?

I have `clasp` working here and can run `clasp push` for the add-on (it'll send the confirm-card fix, the time-fetch hardening, and this diagnostic together). Pushing is a deploy, so I won't do it without your go-ahead — say the word and I'll push, and you handle the engine restart (or tell me how it's run and I'll help). 

My honest read: this is most likely a not-deployed issue, not a code issue — but if the new "Diagnostic: got=false…" message shows up after pushing, then `Events.get` is genuinely failing and I'll fix the time source itself (e.g. a `CalendarApp.getEventById` fallback), rather than keep guessing.

Syntax is clean. Here's where we are and what to do.

## What the log proves

It's **not** a timing/code issue anymore — `Calendar.Events.get` is failing outright. The card can read **attendees** (those ride in on the add-on payload via the `calendar.addons.current.event.*` scopes you granted), but reading the **event resource** (which is the only source of start/end) needs the **`calendar.events.readonly`** scope — and that one was added to the manifest *after* your original authorization. Google doesn't silently re-prompt for new scopes, so your active grant is missing it → `Events.get` throws → no iCalUID, no times → "Could not read the event time."

**1. **2. Re-push the card and hard-reload.** The version running in Calendar is behind the file on disk (it sent `start:null` without showing my new diagnostic). After re-pushing, fully close and reopen the Calendar tab — add-on code is cached for a few minutes.

> AGENT

Confirmed and resolved — and it was never a code bug in the send path.

## Root cause

You were sending from the **event create/edit form**, where the event isn't saved yet. The add-on shows the guest because **attendees come from the panel payload**, but the event's **date/time only exist in the Calendar API once the event is saved** — and `Calendar.Events.get` returns `Not Found` (the 404 in your diagnostic) for an unsaved event. No event record → no time → send fails. On **saved** events it works (which is why it worked for you earlier). The `calendar.events.readonly` scope was a red herring — the `Not Found` (not an auth error) ruled that out.

## What I shipped (pushed at 5:23 pm)

1. **A banner at the top of the panel** when the event can't be read yet: *"Save the event to send passes — passes use the event's date and time, which we can only read once it's saved."* So you see it immediately, not after walking to Send.
2. **A clear send message** ("Save the event first…") replacing the cryptic engine error, for the case where you reach Send anyway.
3. Kept the time-fetch hardening and send-time pass from before (they help saved-but-still-propagating events).

I also removed the temporary engine debug logging (engine reloaded clean, health green).

## How to use it
Create the event → **click Save** → then open the saved event (or reopen the Auxilio panel on it) → **Send passes**. That's the supported flow, and it'll work.

If you'd like, a future enhancement could let hosts send straight from the create form by writing the event via the `currentEventAccess: READ_WRITE` scope (save-then-send in one tap) — but that's a real piece of work, not a quick fix, so I'd scope it separately rather than bolt it on now. Want me to write that up as a plan, or leave the save-first flow as is?

> AGENT

Confirmed it in the git history — here's the real story.

## How it "worked" before

In the earlier calendar→pass code (commit `1f2934b`), `calendar-ingest.ts` had this:

```js
const end = event.end ?? new Date(start.getTime() + 60 * 60 * 1000); // default +1h
```

So when the event's end time was missing, the engine **guessed** (start + 1 hour) and issued the pass anyway. That's why a send could go through without a fully-resolved event.

The **2026-06-17 decision** ("a pass's validity window must equal the event's real start/end — never guess") deliberately removed that fallback. The add-on's send path now goes through `resolveEventWindow`, which returns *nothing* when times are missing rather than inventing them — so it requires the event's real saved time. That change is what made the unsaved-event case start failing. It wasn't a regression by accident; it was a deliberate "don't put a wrong time on a visitor pass" call (a guard at reception checks that window).

## Can we save the event ourselves, then send?

Honestly: **no, not reliably** — and I'd push back on building it. Two hard blockers, both confirmed by today's debugging:

1. **We can't read the time the host typed into an unsaved event.** It's not in the add-on payload (that only carries attendees — that's why the roster shows but the time doesn't), and the Calendar API 404s on an unsaved event. So even if we forced a save, we'd have nothing to validate the pass window against.
2. **There's no API to "save" the compose form.** A Calendar add-on can *modify* the open event (add attendees, etc.) via `currentEventAccess`, but it can't commit the draft the user is composing — the Save button is the user's action.

So "auto-save then send" would require data we fundamentally don't have until the host saves. We'd be back to guessing the time — exactly what we removed on purpose.

## The better UX (my recommendation)

**Keep save-first — but make it a non-event.** Two reasons it's the right call:

- In real use this friction barely exists: **you always save a meeting** — nobody creates a calendar event and leaves it as an unsaved draft. You hit it only because you were testing by sending straight from the create form. The natural flow is: fill event + guests → **Save** (which you'd do anyway) → **Send passes**, and it works.
- The alternative (reintroduce a guessed window so unsaved sends "work") puts wrong times on real visitor passes. That's worse than one Save click, and it undoes a decision you made for a good reason.

So the flow I'd commit to: add guests → Save → send passes on the saved event. The banner I just shipped makes the unsaved state obvious instead of failing at the end.

**One small polish worth doing:** on an unsaved event, relabel the primary button from "Review & send" to **"Save event first"** so it's self-explanatory *before* the click, not just in the banner. Cheap, no downside.

**One alternative I considered and rejected:** show date/time pickers in the card when we can't read the event, and let the host type the visit window. It works, but it's clunky (they already set the time in the event form) and the two can drift. Not worth it.

Want me to apply the button relabel, and double-check whether the panel auto-refreshes the moment you hit Save (the `eventUpdateTrigger`) so the banner clears on its own — or leave it as the banner + guidance you have now?

> DEVELOPER

but when I open a new calendar event, it has time filled in it, just saving it locks that time. why cant we use that time.

> AGENT

That's a genuinely sharp point, and it deserves a precise answer rather than another assertion from me — I've been wrong on specifics this session, and if you're right it's an easy win.

Here's the catch as I understand it: the time **is** in Calendar's UI, but the question is whether Google *hands that time to the add-on*. Google's Calendar add-on trigger payload (`e.calendar`) historically includes only `attendees`, `organizer`, `id`, `calendarId`, `conferenceData` — **not** `start`/`end`. That's why the roster shows (attendees are in the payload) but the time doesn't. It's a platform gap: the time exists in the UI, but it's neither in the data Google sends our code nor readable via the API until you save.

**But I don't want to just claim that — let me verify it on your actual event.** If Google does include the time in the payload, we read it and you never have to save first. I've added a debug dump of exactly what the payload contains:

Definitive: `start=absent end=absent`, and the payload only carries `id | attendees | conferenceData | calendarId | organizer | capabilities`. So your instinct was reasonable, but the verdict is clear — **Google simply does not hand the event's time to the add-on.** The time is in Calendar's UI, but it's neither in the data the platform sends our code nor readable via the API until you save. There is no way to read it from an unsaved event. This isn't a limitation we chose; it's the add-on platform's payload.

566	      .setBottomLabel(included[i].email)
567	      .setWrapText(true));
568	  }
569	  card.addSection(sec);
570	  if (excluded.length) {
571	    var ex = CardService.newCardSection().setHeader('Skipped (no pass / cancelled if sent before)');
572	    for (var j = 0; j < excluded.length; j++) {
573	      ex.addWidget(CardService.newDecoratedText()
574	        .setText(excluded[j].name).setBottomLabel(excluded[j].email).setWrapText(true));
575	    }
576	    card.addSection(ex);
577	  }
578	
579	  // Email section: template picker (only when there's a real choice) + preview. The
580	  // picker is per-send (one template for the whole batch); preview opens the REAL
581	  // branded HTML for the first included guest. Both read/write the same draft, so
582	  // pick → preview → send stays consistent.
583	  var templates = [];
584	  try { templates = JSON.parse(params.templates || '[]'); } catch (err) { templates = []; }
585	  var emailSec = CardService.newCardSection().setHeader('Email');
586	  if (templates.length >= 2) {
587	    var picker = CardService.newSelectionInput()
588	      .setType(CardService.SelectionInputType.DROPDOWN)
589	      .setTitle('Template')
590	      .setFieldName('email_template');
591	    var current = params.currentTemplate || '';
592	    for (var t = 0; t < templates.length; t++) {
593	      var tmpl = templates[t];
594	      var selected = current ? (tmpl.key === current) : (tmpl.isDefault === true);
595	      picker.addItem(tmpl.name || tmpl.key, tmpl.key, selected);
596	    }
597	    emailSec.addWidget(picker);
598	  }
599	  emailSec.addWidget(CardService.newTextButton()
600	    .setText('Preview email')
601	    .setOnClickAction(CardService.newAction().setFunctionName('onPreview').setParameters(params)));
602	  card.addSection(emailSec);
603	
604	  card.setFixedFooter(CardService.newFixedFooter()
605	    .setPrimaryButton(CardService.newTextButton()
606	      .setText('Send passes')
607	      .setTextButtonStyle(CardService.TextButtonStyle.FILLED)
608	      .setBackgroundColor(BRAND_COLOR)
609	      .setOnClickAction(CardService.newAction().setFunctionName('onSaveAndSend').setParameters(params)))
610	    .setSecondaryButton(CardService.newTextButton()
611	      .setText('Back')
612	      .setOnClickAction(CardService.newAction().setFunctionName('onConfirmBack').setParameters(params))));
613	  return card.build();
614	}
615	
616	function onConfirmBack(e) {
617	  return CardService.newActionResponseBuilder()
618	    .setNavigation(CardService.newNavigation().popCard())
619	    .build();
620	}
621	
622	function onSaveAndSend(e) {
623	  var iCalUid = e.parameters.iCalUid;
624	  // Toggles/names/phones were already persisted by onReviewSend on the ROSTER card.
625	  // This runs from the CONFIRM card, which has none of those widgets — re-running
626	  // patchFromForm_ here would read empty inputs and wipe every guest to include=false
627	  // (the "No changes / No pass" bug). Only the (optional) template picker lives here.
628	  patchTemplateFromForm_(iCalUid, e);
629	  // Re-resolve event times here and send them along: if the draft-open missed them
630	  // (flaky Events.get on a just-created event), the draft would have no start/end and
631	  // the send would fail with EVENT_TIME_MISSING. Passing fresh times lets the engine
632	  // self-correct the draft instead of forcing the host to reopen.
633	  var ev = loadEvent_(e);
634	  // Times come only from Calendar.Events.get (the payload has none). If we still can't
635	  // read them, say exactly why instead of the engine's generic message — `got=false`
636	  // means the API call itself failed (scope/Advanced-Service/permission, not a timing
637	  // blip); `got=true start=null` means the event genuinely has no start.
638	  if (!ev.start || !ev.end) {
639	    // A 'Not Found' from Calendar.Events.get means the event isn't saved/propagated
640	    // yet — the panel shows attendees from the payload, but the event record (the only
641	    // source of start/end) doesn't exist server-side. Tell the host plainly.
642	    if (ev.debug.indexOf('Not Found') >= 0 || !ev.gotEvent) {
643	      return notify_('Save the event first (click Save), then reopen this panel and send. The pass time comes from the saved event.');
644	    }
645	    return notify_('Could not read the event time. Diagnostic: ' + ev.debug);
646	  }
647	  var res = engineFetch_('/api/visitor/calendar/addon/send', 'POST', {
648	    iCalUid: iCalUid, start: ev.start, end: ev.end
649	  });
650	  var msg;
651	  if (res.code === 200) {
652	    var sent = (res.json.created || []).length;
653	    var cancelled = (res.json.cancelled || []).length;
654	    var failed = res.json.failed || [];
655	    if (failed.length) {
656	      // Surface WHY a pass couldn't be sent (most often: event time in the past)
657	      // instead of silently leaving the guest pending.
658	      var reason = (failed[0] && failed[0].reason) || 'could not be sent';
659	      msg = "Couldn't send " + failed.length + ' pass' + (failed.length > 1 ? 'es' : '') + ': ' + reason;
660	    } else {
661	      var parts = [];
662	      if (sent) parts.push(sent + ' sent');
663	      if (cancelled) parts.push(cancelled + ' cancelled');
664	      msg = parts.length ? ('Passes updated: ' + parts.join(', ') + '.') : 'No changes.';
665	    }
666	  } else {
667	    msg = 'Update failed (' + res.code + ').';
668	  }
669	  // Re-render so the card switches to Manage mode and shows fresh statuses.
670	  return CardService.newActionResponseBuilder()
671	    .setNotification(CardService.newNotification().setText(msg))
672	    .setNavigation(CardService.newNavigation().updateCard(buildVisitorCard_(e)))
673	    .build();
674	}
675	
676	function onPreview(e) {
677	  var iCalUid = e.parameters.iCalUid;
678	  // Runs from the CONFIRM card (no toggle/name/phone widgets) — persist only the
679	  // template choice; never re-derive include/name/phone here (would wipe the roster).
680	  patchTemplateFromForm_(iCalUid, e);
681	  // The included guests were already resolved on the roster card; onReviewSend threads
682	  // the first one through as previewEmail (the switches aren't on this card to read).
683	  var target = e.parameters.previewEmail || '';
684	  if (!target) return notify_('Turn on at least one guest to preview their email.');
685	
686	  var pv = engineFetch_('/api/visitor/calendar/addon/preview', 'POST', { iCalUid: iCalUid, visitorEmail: target });
687	  if (pv.code !== 200 || !pv.json.previewToken) return notify_('Preview failed (' + pv.code + ').');
688	
689	  // Open the REAL branded HTML in a popup overlay (the card can't render HTML).
690	  var url = ENGINE_BASE + '/api/visitor/calendar/addon/preview-html?t=' + encodeURIComponent(pv.json.previewToken);
691	  return CardService.newActionResponseBuilder()
692	    .setOpenLink(CardService.newOpenLink()
693	      .setUrl(url)
694	      .setOpenAs(CardService.OpenAs.OVERLAY)
695	      .setOnClose(CardService.OnClose.NOTHING))
696	    .build();
697	}
698	
699	// ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────
700	function patchFromForm_(iCalUid, e) {
701	  var emails = (e.parameters.emails || '').split(',').filter(Boolean_);
702	  var inputs = formInputs_(e);
703	  // The template picker lives on the confirm card only; it's absent (empty) when this
704	  // runs from the roster card, so we leave emailTemplateKey untouched there. One choice
705	  // applies to the whole batch (templates are stored per-guest).
706	  var tmpl = formValue_(inputs['email_template']);
707	  var guests = emails.map(function (em) {
708	    var g = {
709	      email: em,
710	      include: formValue_(inputs['inc_' + em]) === 'on', // OFF switches are absent → false
711	      name: formValue_(inputs['name_' + em]),
712	      phone: formValue_(inputs['phone_' + em])
713	    };
714	    if (tmpl) g.emailTemplateKey = tmpl;
715	    return g;
716	  });
717	  return engineFetch_('/api/visitor/calendar/addon/draft', 'PATCH', { iCalUid: iCalUid, patch: { guests: guests } });
718	}
719	
720	// Confirm-card patch: persist ONLY the batch email-template choice. The confirm card
721	// has no include/name/phone widgets, so we must not derive (and overwrite) those here
722	// — only `email_template` is present. Absent/no-change picker → no-op.
723	function patchTemplateFromForm_(iCalUid, e) {
724	  var tmpl = formValue_(formInputs_(e)['email_template']);
725	  if (!tmpl) return; // no picker shown, or nothing to change
726	  var emails = (e.parameters.emails || '').split(',').filter(Boolean_);
727	  if (!emails.length) return;
728	  var guests = emails.map(function (em) { return { email: em, emailTemplateKey: tmpl }; });
729	  return engineFetch_('/api/visitor/calendar/addon/draft', 'PATCH', { iCalUid: iCalUid, patch: { guests: guests } });
730	}
731	
732	function formInputs_(e) {
733	  return (e.commonEventObject && e.commonEventObject.formInputs) || {};
734	}
735	function formValue_(input) {
736	  if (input && input.stringInputs && input.stringInputs.value && input.stringInputs.value.length) {
737	    return input.stringInputs.value[0];
738	  }
739	  return '';
740	}
741	function Boolean_(x) { return !!x; }
742	
743	function textPreview_(json) {
744	  var t = json.text || json.html || '';
745	  t = t.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ').replace(/&nbsp;/g, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
746	  return t.length > 1200 ? t.substring(0, 1200) + '…' : t;
747	}
748	
749	function notify_(msg) {
750	  return CardService.newActionResponseBuilder()
751	    .setNotification(CardService.newNotification().setText(msg))
752	    .build();
753	}
754	
755	function errorCard_(msg) {
756	  return CardService.newCardBuilder()
757	    .setHeader(CardService.newCardHeader()
758	      .setTitle('Something went wrong')
759	      .setImageUrl(ADDON_ICON_URL))
760	    .addSection(CardService.newCardSection()
761	      .addWidget(CardService.newDecoratedText()
762	        .setStartIcon(CardService.newIconImage().setIcon(CardService.Icon.DESCRIPTION))
763	        .setText(msg)
764	        .setWrapText(true)))
765	    .build();
766	}
767	
```

> AGENT

Done. Here's what's now live and the flow to expect.

## What changed
- **Unsaved event:** the panel shows the "Save the event…" banner, and the footer button now reads **"Save event first"** — clicking it just reminds you to save (instead of leading you into the confirm screen and failing at the end).
- **After you click Save:** Google fires the `eventUpdateTrigger`, the panel re-renders, the event is now readable from the API, the banner clears, and the button flips to **"Review & send"** — which works.

So the flow is: **add guest → Save the event → Send passes.** The only "extra" step is Save, which you do anyway to create the meeting.

## Please verify one thing
When you hit **Save** with the panel open, does it **auto-refresh** to "Review & send" on its own? It should (that's the `eventUpdateTrigger`), but I'm not 100% certain Google fires it on the *first* save of a brand-new event. If it doesn't refresh by itself, clicking the event again (reopening the panel) will pick up the saved state. Tell me which happens — if it doesn't auto-refresh, I'll add a small "I've saved — refresh" affordance so it's still one tap.

## On the original question
To close the loop honestly: the time being visible in the create form was a fair thing to expect we could use, but the empirical dump settled it — Google's add-on payload contains `id | attendees | conferenceData | calendarId | organizer | capabilities` and **no start/end**, so the time genuinely isn't available to us until the event is saved. We're not guessing a window (that decision was deliberate, so passes match the meeting), hence save-first.

> AGENT

Good instinct — deferring instead of blocking is the right direction, and it actually respects the "never guess the time" rule (we wait for the real saved time rather than inventing one). But let me pressure-test it honestly, because the idea has two load-bearing assumptions that aren't guaranteed, and one of them we can verify *right now* with the logging I just added.

## The hard parts (where this lives or dies)

**1. Correlation — does the unsaved event's ID survive the save?**
Your plan hinges on "save the calendar event id now, match it to the saved event later." That only works if the ID Google gives us for the *unsaved* event equals the ID after it's saved. If Google mints a new ID on save (very possible for a draft being composed), the link is broken and we can't tell which saved event the scheduled config belongs to. **This is the make-or-break question, and we can answer it with the payload logging already in place:** open the panel on an unsaved event (logs id A), save it, reopen (logs id B). If A == B, your design is viable. If not, we need a different anchor.

**2. The "they saved it" signal.**
The add-on only gets told about the event *while its panel is open* (the update trigger). If the host configures invites, then closes the panel and saves later, our code never runs — so nothing sends. To fulfill after the panel closes, the only reliable trigger is the **backend calendar-watch**, which requires the host's calendar to be **OAuth-connected** (we already have that feature, for move/cancel sync). Without it, "schedule then walk away" silently does nothing.

The "discard if they don't save" part is the easy bit — just expire unsaved scheduled drafts after a TTL.

## A better-engineered version of your idea

Reuse what already exists instead of building a new mechanism:

- **"Schedule invites"** persists the config (we already create a draft on every open) and marks it *pending-on-save*.
- Fulfillment rides the **existing OAuth calendar-watch**: when the host saves, Google pushes the event to the engine, it now has the real time, we match the pending draft and send. Bonus — you get move/cancel lifecycle for free, which is already built.
- **Gate it on calendar connection.** If connected → true fire-and-forget. If not → "Schedule invites" first offers the one-tap Connect (already there), or falls back to save-first.
- **For bulletproof correlation** (sidestepping problem 1): when they tap Schedule, write a marker onto the event via the `currentEventAccess: READ_WRITE` we already hold (e.g. an extended property). It persists through save, so the watch matches on *our* marker, not a possibly-volatile ID. (Needs verifying that add-ons can set extended properties — not certain.)

## My honest recommendation

Two flags first:
- This reintroduces **send-without-a-final-in-panel-confirmation** — the host clicks Schedule, then a normal "Save" fires real passes. That's a deliberate reversal of the "explicit send only" posture from 2026-06-17. Defensible (Schedule *is* the consent), but call it out.
- The juice-vs-squeeze question: the host **saves the meeting anyway**. If we just preserve their config across the save and auto-refresh the panel, the flow is "configure → Save → tap Send" — two taps, *zero* correlation/watch/discard complexity, fully reliable. Your design saves exactly one tap (the final Send) in exchange for a lot of moving parts.

So:
- **If the real scenario is "host configures, then closes the panel and moves on"** → your deferred-send is genuinely worth it, built on the OAuth watch as above.
- **If the host is in the panel through the save anyway** → save-first-with-preserved-config wins on simplicity and reliability, and feels nearly identical.

**Concrete next step:** let's settle problem #1 before designing further — open the panel on an unsaved event, save, reopen, and I'll read the log to see whether the event ID is stable across save. That single fact decides whether "schedule invites" is clean or needs the marker workaround. Want to grab that log now?

```
**After you saved (12:12:54):**

```

## The key finding: the event ID is stable across save

This is the linchpin your idea needed, and it holds:
- The unsaved event's `id` **equals** the saved event's `id`.
- The saved `iCalUID` is just **`<id>@google.com`** — derivable from the unsaved id.

That means **correlation is solved.** We can persist the config now against that id and reliably match it to the saved event later — no fuzzy matching, no marker-writing workaround needed. (It also re-confirms: the payload *never* carries start/end; times come only from the saved-event API resource, which is why it's null until you save.)

## Updated verdict on "Schedule invites"

With correlation no longer a risk, your idea is genuinely viable and clean. Recommended shape:

- **"Schedule invites"** persists the draft config (we already create the draft on open) and flags it `pending-on-save`.
- **Fulfill on the first signal that the event is readable**, whichever arrives:
  - **Panel reopened / re-rendered after save** (the log shows we get full times then) → auto-send, clear the flag.
  - **Backend calendar-watch** (if the host connected their calendar) → fires even if they closed the panel — *true* fire-and-forget, and it reuses the move/cancel lifecycle you already built.
- **Discard:** pending drafts not fulfilled within a TTL (say 24h) get cleaned up.

Two honest caveats:
1. **The "walk away and it still sends" guarantee needs the OAuth calendar connection.** Without it, fulfillment only happens when they reopen the panel after saving (still fine, just not magic). So I'd gate the fire-and-forget promise on connection (one-tap, already exists).
2. It's a deliberate softening of the **2026-06-17 "explicit send only"** stance — "Schedule" becomes the consent, and a normal Save then fires passes with no final in-panel confirm. Defensible, but it's your call since you set that rule.

> AGENT

You're right that read+write calendar OAuth exists in the system — but it's worth being precise about *where*, because it changes what's possible. There are two separate OAuth contexts:

**1. The add-on's own manifest scopes** (`auxilio-workspace/appsscript.json`):
- `calendar.events.readonly` — **read only**
- `calendar.addons.current.event.write` — write, but only to the **currently-open event while the panel is executing**

This write can't run after the panel closes and can't watch for changes. So the add-on *itself* can't power "send after they close the tab."

**2. The engine's "Connect calendar" OAuth** (`office-ops-engine/.../calendar-oauth.ts`):
- `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.events` — **read + write**, scope version `v3-events-rw`

**This** is the read+write you're thinking of. The engine stores a per-user refresh token and runs the background **calendar-watch** with it. This is exactly the piece that makes "Schedule invites → fulfill after the panel is closed" work: host saves the event → Google pushes the change → the watch ingests the saved event (real time + `iCalUID`) → we match the pending-schedule draft (by `<id>@google.com`) → send.

So your idea is **fully buildable on infrastructure that already exists** — the watch, the per-user token, the draft store, and (now confirmed) the stable event ID for correlation.

Two refinements, not blockers:
- **It only kicks in for hosts who've completed the one-time "Connect calendar" step.** The add-on offers that button today, but it's not automatic on install. So "Schedule invites" would either require/prompt that connection, or fall back to fulfilling when the host reopens the panel.
- **We don't even need the *write* half for this.** Deferred-send only *reads* the saved event's time, then issues passes in our own system — no calendar write. So even an older read-only connection is enough; write only matters for the unrelated Slack "create event on a host's calendar" path.

The one engine change of substance: the watch ingest is currently **draft-only** (the 2026-06-17 no-auto-send rule). "Schedule invites" adds a narrow, explicitly-consented exception — *if a draft is flagged pending-schedule, the ingest materializes and sends it* (then clears the flag) — rather than reversing no-auto-send broadly.

Want me to write this up as an implementation plan? I'd cover: the "Schedule invites" UI + connection prompt, the `pending-schedule` flag on the draft, the ingest-path send-on-match, ID/iCalUID correlation, TTL cleanup, and how it stays consistent with the existing lifecycle (move/cancel) and the no-auto-send posture.

449	        return false;

460	      status: 'cancelled',
461	      providerEventId: event.providerEventId,
462	    });
463	    await emitIngested(event, tenantId, ids, 'cancelled');
464	    return { status: 'cancelled', iCalUid: linkUid, invitationIds: ids };
465	  }
466	
467	  const startISO = event.start ? event.start.toISOString() : null;
468	  const endISO = event.end ? event.end.toISOString() : null;

470	  // ── Update to an event we've already seen ──

480	    const oldIds = parseInvitationIds(existing.invitation_ids);
481	    const activeOldIds = new Set(await filterActiveInvitationIds(oldIds).catch(() => oldIds));
482	    for (const id of oldIds) {

484	    }
485	
486	    // Draft-authoritative events re-issue from the host's CURATED roster, not raw
487	    // attendees — otherwise a reschedule would wipe the host's customization. Every
488	    // other (non-curated) event takes the original path below, unchanged.
489	    let rescheduledIds: string[];
490	    let rescheduleReason: string | undefined;
491	    if (existing.draft_authoritative) {
492	      const draft = await getDraft(linkUid);
493	      if (draft) {
494	        // Don't reissue a guest whose pass was externally cancelled before the move:
495	        // a guest who HAD a pass (status 'sent' + id) that's no longer active is
496	        // excluded from the reissue. New/uncancelled included guests are unaffected.
497	        for (const g of draft.roster) {
498	          if (g.status === 'sent' && g.invitationId && !activeOldIds.has(g.invitationId)) {
499	            g.include = false;
500	          }
501	        }
502	      }
503	      if (draft && draft.roster.some((g) => g.include)) {
504	        const mat = await materializeDraft(

506	          { rescheduled: true },
507	        );
508	        rescheduledIds = mat.invitationIds;
509	        await markMaterialized(linkUid, mat.passes, {
510	          eventStart: startISO ?? undefined,
511	          eventEnd: endISO ?? undefined,
512	        });
513	      } else {
514	        rescheduledIds = [];
515	        rescheduleReason = 'draft has no included guests';
516	      }
517	    } else if (activeOldIds.size > 0) {
518	      // Tracked event with linked invites but NO draft = the Slack-self-host D8
519	      // path (event created via the host's OAuth). Reissue ONLY the still-active
520	      // invitations at the new time from the invitation record itself.
521	      const newIds: string[] = [];
522	      for (const oldId of oldIds) {

526	        if (fresh) newIds.push(fresh);

540	      sequence: event.sequence,

543	      invitationIds: rescheduledIds,

572	/**
573	 * No-auto-send model (2026-06-17): classify attendees and build (or refresh) the
574	 * Visit Draft for the host to curate + send in the add-on. Does NOT create invites.
575	 * Returns `drafted: false` with a reason when the event isn't an in-person visit.
576	 */
577	async function draftFromEvent(
578	  event: NormalizedCalendarEvent,
579	  domains: string[],
580	  tenantId: string,
581	): Promise<{ drafted: boolean; reason?: string }> {
582	  const { host, visitors } = classifyAttendees(event, domains);
583	
584	  if (!host?.email) return { drafted: false, reason: 'no organizer/host' };
585	  // Host must be internal — otherwise this is an outside party inviting our
586	  // mailbox, not an employee hosting a visit. Forgery/irrelevance guard.
587	  if (domains.length && !domains.includes(domainOf(host.email))) {
588	    return { drafted: false, reason: `organizer ${host.email} not on an internal domain` };
589	  }
590	  if (visitors.length === 0) {
591	    return { drafted: false, reason: 'no external attendees (not an in-person visit)' };
592	  }
593	
594	  // Build the draft from the classified visitors (host already excluded). Non-
595	  // destructive: getOrCreateDraft keeps any roster the host has already curated.

598	    tenantId,

600	    title: event.title,
601	    hostEmail: host.email,
602	    attendees: visitors,
603	    eventStart: event.start?.toISOString(),
604	    eventEnd: event.end?.toISOString(),
605	    location: event.location,
606	  });
607	  // Keep times fresh on redelivery/reschedule without clobbering host roster edits.
608	  await touchDraftTimes(event.iCalUid, event.start?.toISOString(), event.end?.toISOString());
609	  return { drafted: true };

but our extension will not work if the user creates an event and closes without the managem notification gets time to appear?

> AGENT

Good — that's exactly the right thing to poke at before I write the plan, because it exposes what this feature can and can't do. Let me be precise about it.

## The hard truth about Calendar add-ons

The add-on (Apps Script) **only runs while its panel is open and the user interacts**. It cannot run on its own. So if the host creates an event and never opens Auxilio — or closes before the card finishes loading — the add-on does nothing. That's a platform wall, not something we can code around.

The *only* thing that can act without the panel is the **backend calendar-watch** (the OAuth connection). That's why it's central to everything here.

## What actually happens in each case

| Host action | What happens |
|---|---|
| Opens panel, taps **Schedule invites**, then closes (even before saving) | **Works.** The intent is persisted **server-side the instant they tap Schedule** — the panel doesn't need to stay open. The watch fulfills on save. |
| Opens panel, card loads, but closes without tapping anything | A draft exists server-side (the card's load call creates it), but **no send** — they never expressed intent. |
| Never opens the panel / closes before the card loads | The add-on did nothing. **If their calendar is connected, the watch still builds a draft on save** — but it won't send (no-auto-send). |

So "Schedule invites" **does** survive "configure then close" — the tap is the commit, and it's stored immediately. What it can't survive is the host **never tapping anything at all**.

## The part that's unavoidable (and correct)

The host has to opt in **once per meeting** — open the panel and tap Schedule/Send. We *cannot* remove that, and we shouldn't: the alternative is auto-sending visitor passes to every external guest of every meeting, which is spam and is exactly the behavior we deliberately turned off on 2026-06-17. The system can't guess which meetings warrant a physical visitor pass; only the host knows.

## The real fix for "host never opened the panel"

That case isn't solved by the add-on at all — it's solved by a **proactive nudge** from the backend watch: when a connected host saves a meeting with an external guest, the engine sends them a Slack/email message — *"Meeting with an outside guest — send a visitor pass? [Open]"* — with a link back into the flow. That reaches hosts who'd otherwise never open the panel, without auto-sending.

So the robust picture is two complementary pieces, both riding the watch:
1. **Schedule invites** — for hosts who *do* open the panel: one tap, fulfilled on save.
2. **Proactive nudge** — for hosts who *don't*: the watch pings them with a link.

## Before I write the plan

Given this, do you want the plan to cover **just "Schedule invites,"** or **"Schedule invites" + the proactive nudge** as one coherent watch-based feature? My recommendation: include the nudge — it's the actual answer to the gap you just identified, and it reuses the same watch + draft plumbing. Either way, both depend on the host having connected their calendar, which is the one prerequisite worth making prominent in the UX.

1	# auxilio-extension
2	
3	Google **Chrome extension (MV3)** for Auxilio (Office Ops) — the **desktop** surface for registering office visitors directly inside Google Calendar. It is the richer counterpart to the `auxilio-workspace` Calendar add-on: where the add-on is mobile-capable but lives under the CardService "card ceiling," the extension gives a full React UI (realtime guest updates, per-guest invite toggle, template preview, inline edits, intentional auto-nudge).
4	
5	> Read the umbrella `../CLAUDE.md` for the four constraints and UX rules — they apply here.
6	> Strategy + approval analysis: `../Planning-docs/2026-06-18_calendar_visitor_chrome_extension_decision.md`.
7	> Build plan: the approved plan that scaffolded this repo.
8	
9	## What this is (and isn't)
10	
11	- **Is:** an MV3 extension (WXT + React 19 + TS) that injects a trigger into Google Calendar (web) and renders the registration flow in the **side panel**. A **channel surface / dumb adapter** — it forwards intent to `office-ops-engine`.
12	- **Isn't:** a place for business logic. Dedup, draft curation, invite/pass issuance, and lifecycle all live in `office-ops-engine`'s visitor plugin. This repo renders UI and calls the engine.
13	- **Isn't:** mobile. Extensions are desktop-Chrome only. The `auxilio-workspace` add-on remains the mobile surface. Both key off the same **magic address**.
14	
15	## Architecture — three MV3 surfaces
16	
17	- **`entrypoints/background.ts`** (service worker) — the only holder of tokens + network. Mints/caches the Google `id_token` (engine auth) + `access_token` (Calendar read), runs the Calendar API client and the engine `/addon/*` client, keeps per-tab event context, routes messages.
18	- **`entrypoints/calendar.content.ts`** (content script @ `calendar.google.com`) — a **dumb trigger/observer**: detect open event, decode `eid`, inject the "Register a visitor" button (Phase 1) and the magic-address banner (Phase 2). No tokens, no business logic.
19	- **`entrypoints/sidepanel/`** (the app) — React + TanStack Query UI. Pure presentation + server state.
20	
21	```
22	content script ──msg──> background ──HTTPS(id_token)──> office-ops-engine /api/visitor/calendar/addon/*
23	side panel    ──msg──> background ──Calendar API(access_token)──> canonical iCalUID + attendees
24	```
25	
26	## Hard rules
27	
28	1. **Calendar-only scopes. Never add Gmail/Drive.** Calendar is *sensitive* (verification, no paid audit). Gmail/Drive are *restricted* → annual CASA assessment. Hard line, same as the add-on.
29	2. **Engine owns the contract.** The extension calls the existing `/api/visitor/calendar/addon/{draft,preview,send,cancel-guest}` routes (ID-token authed). Do **not** add engine signature/scope changes from here. The only engine-side change is env: add this extension's OAuth client id to `AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` (audience allow-list).
30	3. **Background is the only secret/network holder.** Content script stays a dumb trigger; side panel stays presentation. Keep the thin-adapter boundary.
31	4. **Send the canonical `iCalUID`** (from the Calendar API `events.get`, not synthesized) so the extension path converges on the same `calendar_event_links` row as `.ics`/OAuth/add-on.
32	5. **Design: Material 3 tokens, not its code.** Our own `design/tokens.ts` + `global.css` (primary `#92288E`). No MUI/Tailwind/paper. **Icons: lucide-react only**, no Unicode glyphs/emoji in chrome.
33	6. **Animations subtle, only where needed** — CSS transitions driven by motion tokens; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.
34	7. **Internal-first.** Force-installed/unlisted to our Workspace → no OAuth verification yet. Public/commercial = a later packaging + verification exercise (CWS review + OAuth verification), per the decision doc.
35	
36	## Stack & commands
37	
38	WXT (MV3 over Vite) + React 19 + TypeScript + TanStack Query (server state) + Zustand (UI/session/event-context) + lucide-react.
39	
40	```bash
41	npm install
42	npm run dev        # WXT dev server; load .output/chrome-mv3 in chrome://extensions (Developer mode → Load unpacked)
43	npm run build      # production build → .output/chrome-mv3
44	npm run compile    # tsc --noEmit (type-check)
45	npm run zip        # packaged zip for store upload
46	```
47	
48	Env: copy `.env.example` → `.env` (engine URL, OAuth client id, magic address). `WXT_` vars are inlined at build.
49	
50	## Deploy / distribution
51	
52	**Not Bifrost, not EAS.** Internal-first via Chrome admin **force-install** (unlisted, by extension id) or "Load unpacked" for dev. Public later via the Chrome Web Store (payments handled by our own engine; CWS no longer bills).
53	
```
